   <div class="inv-intro">
   <p>Every piece of data the SimBA team has shared publicly, and where it is: pose-estimation models, labelled images, videos, behavior classifiers, training data, example projects and talks. It covers 28 OSF projects on the <a href="https://osf.io/user/mutws" target="_blank" rel="noopener">sgoldenlab OSF account</a>, one Google Drive folder and the data files inside the SimBA GitHub repository.</p>
   <div class="inv-stats">
   <div><b>110</b><span>datasets &amp; files</span></div>
   <div><b>116,387</b><span>files inside archives</span></div>
   <div><b>298 GB</b><span>on OSF + Drive</span></div>
   <div><b>2026-10-15</b><span>snapshot date</span></div>
   </div>
   <nav class="inv-nav"><a href="#start-here">Start here</a><a href="#all-data">All data (search)</a><a href="#browse-by-location">Browse by location</a><a href="#data-files-in-the-simba-github-repository">Files in the GitHub repo</a><a href="#about-this-page">About this page</a></nav>
   </div>

Start here
----------

.. raw:: html

   <div class="inv-wrap"><table class="inv-table inv-start"><thead><tr><th>I want to…</th><th>Use</th></tr></thead><tbody>
   <tr><td><b>Try SimBA end to end on a small dataset</b></td><td><a href="https://osf.io/dg385/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Tutorial Data</a>: 3 short videos with their tracking data, annotated <code>targets_inserted</code> files and two classifiers (attack, defensive), 2.7 GB.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Open a finished SimBA project</b></td><td><a href="#d86" class="inv-jump">Mouse resident-intruder example project (attack)</a>, 2.3 GB.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Track two mice (white + black) in a home cage</b></td><td>DeepLabCut models, 16 body parts: <a href="#d1" class="inv-jump">CLAHE</a>, <a href="#d2" class="inv-jump">GREYSCALE</a>, <a href="#d3" class="inv-jump">RGB</a>.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Track one mouse in an open field or operant box</b></td><td><a href="#d5" class="inv-jump">open field model</a>, <a href="#d6" class="inv-jump">operant model</a>.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Track two rats</b></td><td><a href="#d8" class="inv-jump">rat resident-intruder model</a>.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Score mouse resident-intruder behaviors</b></td><td>Classifiers and per-behavior annotations on <a href="https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/17-Bi0D78Qn--tyR_zWBxy7bIpawCuWL4" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Google Drive</a> (16 body parts, 498 features; see the note below the table). Older OSF classifiers use 490 features and are not interchangeable with these.</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Score rat resident-intruder or CRIM13 behaviors</b></td><td><a href="#d85" class="inv-jump">rat training data</a>, <a href="#d84" class="inv-jump">CRIM13 training data</a>, and the matching classifiers: <a href="#all-data" class="inv-jump" data-set-type="classifier">show all classifiers</a>. <span class="inv-sub">CRIM13 videos come from the Caltech Resident-Intruder Mouse dataset (Burgos-Artizzu, Doll&aacute;r, Lin, Anderson &amp; Perona, &ldquo;Social behavior recognition in continuous video&rdquo;, CVPR 2012); please cite it when you use this data.</span></td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Train your own pose model</b></td><td><a href="#all-data" class="inv-jump" data-set-type="labelled">Labelled images</a> (PNG frames + body-part annotations).</td></tr>
   <tr><td><b>Get raw experiment videos</b></td><td><a href="#all-data" class="inv-jump" data-set-type="videos">Videos</a>.</td></tr>
   </tbody></table></div>
   <div class="inv-callout"><b>Before you download a classifier (.sav):</b> they were saved with scikit-learn 0.22 and only load in SimBA&rsquo;s Python 3.6 install (tested). On Python 3.9+ installs, retrain from the training data instead (<a href="Scenario3.html">Scenario 3</a>).</div>

   <p class="inv-note">*OSF&rsquo;s own download counter for each file since upload, summed over identical copies. For a split archive it is the lowest count among its parts (a download needs every part). OSF does not say whether repeat or automated downloads are filtered, so read it as rough popularity. Google Drive publishes no counts. Complete file lists of every archive in one file: <a href="_static/data_inventory/all_archive_contents.csv.gz" download>all_archive_contents.csv.gz</a> (gzip-compressed CSV; unpack with 7-Zip or <code>gunzip</code>). MD5 and SHA-256 of every OSF file, to verify downloads: <a href="_static/data_inventory/checksums.csv" download>checksums.csv</a>.</p>
   <script>
   (function(){
     var q=document.getElementById('inv-q'), rows=[].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('#inv-all > tbody > tr')), cnt=document.getElementById('inv-count');
     var state={type:'',species:''};
     function apply(){
       var words=(q.value||'').toLowerCase().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean), shown=0;
       rows.forEach(function(r){
         var ok=(!state.type||r.dataset.type===state.type)&&(!state.species||r.dataset.species===state.species)&&
                words.every(function(w){return r.dataset.search.indexOf(w)>-1;});
         r.style.display=ok?'':'none'; if(ok)shown++;
       });
       cnt.textContent='Showing '+shown+' of '+rows.length+' rows';
     }
     function setChip(group,val){
       state[group]=val;
       [].forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll('.inv-chips[data-group="'+group+'"] button'),function(b){b.classList.toggle('on',(b.dataset[group]||'')===val);});
       apply();
     }
     [].forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll('.inv-chips button'),function(b){
       b.addEventListener('click',function(){var g=b.parentNode.dataset.group; setChip(g,b.dataset[g]||'');});
     });
     [].forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll('a[data-set-type]'),function(a){
       a.addEventListener('click',function(){q.value=''; setChip('species',''); setChip('type',a.dataset.setType);});
     });
     [].forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll('a.inv-jump[href^="#d"]'),function(a){
       a.addEventListener('click',function(){q.value=''; setChip('species',''); setChip('type','');
         var t=document.querySelector(a.getAttribute('href')); if(t){t.classList.add('inv-hit'); setTimeout(function(){t.classList.remove('inv-hit');},2500);}});
     });
     q.addEventListener('input',apply); apply();
   })();
   </script>

.. raw:: html

   <div class="inv-callout" id="mouse-ri-note"><b>About the mouse resident-intruder training data (Google Drive).</b> Checked from the column headers of two annotation CSVs: each has 548 columns = 1 index + 48 pose columns (16 body parts × x, y, probability) + 498 feature columns + 1 behavior column (0/1). The body parts are Nose, Ear_left, Ear_right, Center, Lat_left, Lat_right, Tail_base and Tail_end for animal 1 and animal 2. The Drive classifiers also expect 498 features (tested <code>Allogroom_vigorous.sav</code>). To reuse this data, your pose estimation must track the same 8 body parts per animal; otherwise the feature columns will not line up.</div>

Data files in the SimBA GitHub repository
-----------------------------------------

.. raw:: html

About this page
---------------

.. raw:: html

   <p class="inv-note">OSF projects by other labs that the SimBA docs link to (not SimBA data): <a href="https://osf.io/e3dyc/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">AMBER-pipeline</a> (linked from <a href="https://github.com/sgoldenlab/simba/blob/master/docs/example_datasets.rst#L117" target="_blank" rel="noopener">docs/example_datasets.rst:117</a>, <a href="https://github.com/sgoldenlab/simba/blob/master/docs/example_datasets.rst#L118" target="_blank" rel="noopener">docs/example_datasets.rst:118</a>, <a href="https://github.com/sgoldenlab/simba/blob/master/docs/example_datasets.rst#L119" target="_blank" rel="noopener">docs/example_datasets.rst:119</a>, +2 more).</p>

Built on 2026-10-15 from read-only crawls: the OSF API (every project, sub-project, wiki and file of the sgoldenlab account, including each file's download counter and SHA-256 checksum), the public Google Drive folder view, and the index at the end of each zip archive, fetched with HTTP range requests so no archive was downloaded. Body-part lists come from the ``CollectedData`` annotation files inside the labelled-image archives. Links into the SimBA repository were found by scanning the docs, README and code. Identical copies are matched by checksum. Classifier training settings come from the SimBA meta files on OSF, matched to classifiers by file name. Column lists come from the header row of one CSV per training dataset. SimBA body-part settings are matched against ``simba/pose_configurations`` by comparing body-part names (ignoring Lateral/Lat spelling and word order within a name). Data files inside the GitHub repository were listed with ``git ls-files``. Re-run the crawlers and ``build_data_inventory.py`` to refresh it.

**What this page cannot see:** private OSF projects or data under other OSF accounts; Google Drive, Dropbox or other folders that are not linked from the SimBA repository; and whether every file inside an archive extracts cleanly (only the archive indexes were read; checking contents would mean downloading about 300 GB).
